Exposure-boundary source review, 13:25 Pacific. Two independent source reviews
and coordinator inspection support a narrower, conditional conclusion about
this stopped invocation without opening its HDF5 contents. The
[composition](converter_training/tools/finding_a_first_readout.py) writes all
38 selected layers and calls `seal_decoded()` before invoking the first
held-out point scorer and subsequent bootstrap. The
[collection writer](converter_training/tools/finding_a_collection.py) uses the
same `.partial` file through HDF5's sec2 driver, creates uncompressed float32
prediction datasets, verifies every unit was written and flushes each layer
and the seal. The [prediction code](converter_training/tools/finding_a_prediction.py)
does not compute truth-comparison scores; the truth reader is lazy.

The fixed shape constants contain 14861288 units per stream. Both streams'
mandatory predictions alone require at least
`2 × 14861288 × 224 × 4 = 26631428096` bytes before scoring; masks, coordinates,
moments and HDF5 overhead add to this bound. The preserved file's logical size,
14628406296 bytes, is smaller. Fully written and flushed uncompressed chunks
cannot fit that required payload in the observed logical file length; sparse
physical allocation does not reduce logical length. Under the submitted-source
and preserved-output identity assumptions and ordinary coherent filesystem
behavior, this cancelled first readout therefore did not reach held-out score
computation. Absence of a final report alone would not establish that result.

At20:25:18UTC, bounded metadata inspection found the wrapper, runner,
composition, collection, prediction, bootstrap and shape-constant source files
regular, with modification/change times preceding the job's start. The partial
file remained14628406296 bytes with modification/change times20:13:57UTC,
before the batch ended. This supports, but is not an independent historical
proof of, the source/file identity assumptions. No payload, HDF5 attributes,
model values, CV magnitudes or scores were read. Test neural responses were
already processed to generate predictions; this source-based stage inference
does not establish that held-out inputs remained unread, resolve other prior

Root and independent source review confirm `ContentCudaPairBudget.check()`
checks the training process's historical RUSAGE_SELF.ru_maxrss against15GiB,
not just current RSS. Checks bracket model construction, feature/cache work
and each update; the progress callback also checks immediately after writing.
A final progress line alone is insufficient to prove survival of that check,
but continued later progress requires more successful checks. All19 queued
content launchers inherit this same guard unchanged. Canonical initialization
constructs one model at a time and retains compact RNG states, not20 models.
The eventual operational report will record the training process's own peak.

The [official sstat documentation](https://slurm.schedmd.com/sstat.html)
describes step/task accounting and plugin-dependent metrics. The
[official cgroup-v2 accounting notes](https://slurm.schedmd.com/cgroup_v2.html)
state that file cache can contribute to RSS/MaxRSS unless excluded by the
site's accounting setting. That is a possible explanation, not a verified
Sherlock configuration or attribution of this peak. No system configuration
was probed. The step-level observation cannot by itself establish that the
training Python process exceeded its15GiB contract.

and frozen scripts. No resource amendment, retry, added test or new allocation

## 2026-10-06 23:27 Pacific — pipeline_conformance tagged converter loss correction prepared

A targeted review of the future converter implementation found an actual
source-fidelity error. In the authenticated V1.0.0 archive, member
`NCC_content_loss/NCC_train.py` line 202 constructs
`torch.nn.MSELoss(reduction='sum')`; line 219 passes it to
`chunk_process_batch`, whose lines 168–171 apply that criterion and sum the
weighted block losses. `utils.py` lines 185–192 calculate normalized inverse
squared feature-block norms without dividing by block size. The local
`feature_protocol.py` fixture instead supplied default mean MSE. Coordinator
source inspection and two independent static reviews confirmed the discrepancy.

The September 30 decision explicitly adopts the pinned code expressions; no
mean-reduction override was adopted. This correction restores that decision.
For block element counts N_l, the tagged objective is sum_l w_l SSE_l; the
incorrect interpretation is sum_l w_l SSE_l/N_l. Unequal convolutional and
fully connected block sizes make this a change in relative layer weighting.
The historical fixture's equal-sized blocks failed to expose that difference.

**Affected evidence:** attempt-008's converter-loss fidelity assertion and
the two mean-MSE descriptions in the historical protocol lock are superseded
as valid evidence for future content-converter execution. Their original bytes
remain preserved. The targeted correction report below must pass before that
branch can be released. Unchanged architecture/normalization evidence remains
usable. Decoder fitting uses its separate alpha-100 ridge objective and does
not call this loss; completed or running fits and VGG feature exports are not
invalidated and are not repeated.

The corrected fixture now binds the source caller's constructor and actual
helper argument, uses six-element versus two-element blocks, and checks the
analytic summed loss **48/7** and per-element gradients **8/7** and **12/7**.
Four focused caller regression tests address the observed defect. These and
the corrected numerical fixture are **unrun**. The new
[`run_converter_loss_correction.py`](pipeline_conformance/tools/run_converter_loss_correction.py)
publishes a separate report at
`pipeline_conformance/converter_loss_correction_001/report.json`; it neither
rewrites the lock nor activates converter fitting. ASTRA now requires this
specific correction output for the future converter stage.

The next eligible fresh refill, work_0011 / global index 10, is prepared to
host these checks in a separate **240-second** child: four caller tests,
